Hi. Thank you so much for seeing me, I really appreciate you taking the time. 

Um... I guess I'm here because... well, I've been a little bit on edge lately? Actually, it's—it's more than that, and it's really embarrassing to admit. I keep sort of... blowing up at people over absolutely nothing. I work in retail, and the other day I actually yelled at one of my employees just for putting some stock on the wrong shelf.

Um... avoiding things? Well... I guess maybe a little bit? But it's just because I've been so busy with work, really. 

Although... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest—and I feel so silly even saying this out loud—I haven't really been going back to my house very much. I've been staying with a friend. Um... well, it's just... I guess I've been staying away from the house a lot lately. I know that probably sounds silly, a grown woman avoiding her own home, but... I just can't seem to make myself go back there right now.

Um... well, it was about two weeks ago. We had just started working on the house—it was our first weekend really getting into it. And... um... there was a fire. In the kitchen. 

I know it probably sounds like I'm overreacting, staying away for two whole weeks just because of a kitchen fire. I just... I haven't been able to shake it since that day. I'm really trying, though. I promise I'm not just giving up. I hope you don't think I'm being completely ridiculous about this... is it okay that it's taking me a little time to get over it?

Um... yes, definitely. It's the fire. I mean, I know I already mentioned staying away from the house, and the mortgage folder... even my patio herbs. I just... I can't go near any of it. 

I know it sounds so weak, and I really don't want you to think I'm just running away from my problems. But any little thing tied to that day... my chest just gets so tight, and I freeze up. I just can't make myself go near any of it right now.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no, definitely not. I mean, my husband was there with me, and I would never shut him out. I'm really trying to make sure I'm still being a good partner, you know?

Um... right after? I think... I think I was okay, mostly.

I don't want you to think I'm just running away from my problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing anything over and over to try and calm yourself or make yourself feel better?\n\nPatient: Um... over and over? I definitely don't have any weird habits or anything I'm relying on like that. I really want to make sure I'm coping the right way, so... I hope I'm doing okay there. Is that the right kind of answer?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Sad? Um... no, not really. I mean, I definitely wouldn't say I'm depressed or anything like that. I try really hard to keep a smile on my face, especially at work. You have to be upbeat for the customers, you know? \n\nI guess... well, maybe I've been a little more tired than usual? And sometimes I just feel sort of... flat, I guess. Everyone gets a little down when they're stressed. \n\nI really try to focus on the positive things and count my blessings. I definitely don't want to sit around feeling sorry for myself. I'm doing my best to stay cheerful, I really am. I hope that's what you're looking for... I just can't seem to make myself go back there right now. \n\nIt's not just the house, either. It's just that... whenever I get near anything that reminds me of what happened, my chest gets so tight I can hardly breathe, and I just... I can't do it. \n\nI know I need to get over it and go home, and I promise I'm trying to work up the courage. I'm not trying to be difficult or run away from my problems, I swear. Is it... is it normal to feel this way? I just want to make sure I'm not completely failing at handling this.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me what happened on the day that's been making it hard for you to go home?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it was about two weeks ago. In the kitchen. \n\nI know it probably sounds like I'm overreacting, staying away for two whole weeks just because of a kitchen fire. I mean, the fire department came and put it out, and nobody was hurt, so I really should just be grateful and move on, right? I hope you don't think I'm being completely ridiculous about this... is it okay that it's taking me a little time to get over it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the fire in your kitchen was the main reason you've been avoiding your home?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, definitely. It's the fire. I mean, I know I already mentioned staying away from the house, and the mortgage folder... even my patio herbs. I just... I can't go near any of it. \n\nI know it sounds so weak, and I really don't want you to think I'm just running away from my problems. But any little thing tied to that day... my chest just gets so tight, and I freeze up. I just can't make myself go near any of it right now. \n\nI'm really trying to be brave about it, I promise. I know I should be handling this better. Is it... does that make sense, that the fire would make me avoid all those other things too? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other places or situations, besides your home, that you've been avoiding since the fire?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. It's just the house and... and the things connected to it, like I mentioned. \n\nI mean, I'm still going to work at the store, and I can go to the grocery store and run my errands just fine. I don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart or anything. I'm really trying to keep the rest of my life as normal as possible. \n\nI guess I just... I haven't noticed anywhere else bothering me like the house does. I'm doing my best to stay functional everywhere else, I swear. Is that... is that a good sign? That it's mostly just the house I'm having trouble with?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any memories or images of the fire that come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I think I was okay, mostly. \n\nI mean, obviously it was a shock, but I really tried to just hold it together. Um... no, I don't think so. Not really. I mean, I think about work a lot, making sure the schedule is right and the floor looks good, but that's just being responsible, right? I try to stay really organized. \n\nI wouldn't say I have thoughts that I can't stop. I can usually distract myself if I need to. I really try not to let my mind run away with things, because I know how important it is to stay positive and focused. Does that make sense? I just... But lately, anything that reminds me of... of that day... it just makes my chest feel so tight, and I just can't seem to force myself to go near any of it. I'm really trying to push past it, I promise. I know it's not a healthy way to cope. Is it okay that I'm struggling with that a little bit? I don't want you to think I'm just running away from my problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing anything over and over to try and calm yourself or make yourself feel better?\n\nPatient: Um... over and over? No, I don't think so. I definitely don't do anything strange or out of the ordinary, I promise. \n\nI try to just... take a few deep breaths if I'm feeling too tense, like people say you're supposed to do. Sometimes at work I'll maybe reorganize a clothing rack or tidy up a display just to keep my hands busy for a second, but that's just being productive, right? I definitely don't have any weird habits or anything I'm relying on like that. I really want to make sure I'm coping the right way, so... I hope I'm doing okay there. Is that the right kind of answer?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Sad? Um... no, not really. I mean, I definitely wouldn't say I'm depressed or anything like that. I try really hard to keep a smile on my face, especially at work. You have to be upbeat for the customers, you know? \n\nI guess... well, maybe I've been a little more tired than usual? And sometimes I just feel sort of... flat, I guess. Like things that usually make me happy just don't have the same spark right now. But I'm sure that's just because I haven't been sleeping great and I'm stressed, right? Everyone gets a little down when they're stressed. \n\nI really try to focus on the positive things and count my blessings. I definitely don't want to sit around feeling sorry for myself. I'm doing my best to stay cheerful, I really am. I hope that's what you're looking for... I just want to make sure I'm handling this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your anxiety?\n\nPatient: Rituals? Um... no, definitely not. I think... maybe that's kind of like what you were just asking a minute ago, right? About doing things over and over? \n\nI promise I don't have anything like that going on. I don't feel forced to do any specific little routines or anything. Just, you know, keeping my hands busy straightening up the store like I mentioned, but that's just being a good employee. I definitely don't have any strange behaviors or anything I'm hiding from you, I swear. \n\nI hope it's okay that I don't have anything like that to report? I really want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to figure all this out, so... I hope I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by 'that day' that's been making it hard for you to go home?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's just... I guess I've been staying away from the house a lot lately. I know that probably sounds silly, a grown woman avoiding her own home, but... I just can't seem to make myself go back there right now. \n\nIt's not just the house, either. It's... well, I haven't even looked at the mortgage folder, and I've completely ignored my little patio herb garden, which I usually love tending to. I know I should be keeping up with my responsibilities, I really do. It's just that... whenever I get near anything that reminds me of what happened, my chest gets so tight I can hardly breathe, and I just... I can't do it. \n\nI know I need to get over it and go home, and I promise I'm trying to work up the courage. I'm not trying to be difficult or run away from my problems, I swear. Is it... is it normal to feel this way? I just want to make sure I'm not completely failing at handling this.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me what happened on the day that's been making it hard for you to go home?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it was about two weeks ago. We had just started working on the house—it was our first weekend really getting into it. And... um... there was a fire. In the kitchen. \n\nI know it probably sounds like I'm overreacting, staying away for two whole weeks just because of a kitchen fire. I mean, the fire department came and put it out, and nobody was hurt, so I really should just be grateful and move on, right? I'm sure other people bounce back from this sort of thing much faster. \n\nI just... I haven't been able to shake it since that day. I'm really trying, though. I promise I'm not just giving up. I hope you don't think I'm being completely ridiculous about this... is it okay that it's taking me a little time to get over it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the fire in your kitchen was the main reason you've been avoiding your home?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, definitely. It's the fire. I mean, I know I already mentioned staying away from the house, and the mortgage folder... even my patio herbs. I just... I can't go near any of it. \n\nI know it sounds so weak, and I really don't want you to think I'm just running away from my problems. But any little thing tied to that day... my chest just gets so tight, and I freeze up. I just can't make myself go near any of it right now. \n\nI'm really trying to be brave about it, I promise. It's just the house and... and the things connected to it, like I mentioned. \n\nI mean, I'm still going to work at the store, and I can go to the grocery store and run my errands just fine. I can smell the smoke, and I see the kitchen filling up with it, and it happens before I can even stop myself. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I shouldn't be letting it interrupt my day like that. I really try to push it away as soon as it happens, I promise. I don't want to be one of those people who just dwells on things. Does... does that happen to other people too? The memories just popping up like that? I hope you don't think I'm just letting my imagination run wild.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any nightmares about the fire?\n\nPatient: Um... no, actually. No nightmares. I mean, I've been sleeping okay, I think. \n\nI really try to clear my head before bed, you know? I don't want to bring all that stress into my sleep. So, no, I haven't been waking up terrified or anything like that. I'm really trying to make sure I get good rest so I can keep functioning at work. \n\nI guess that's a positive thing, right? I really hope that shows I'm handling things somewhat well... at least at night.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding people who were with you during the fire?\n\nPatient: Um... no, definitely not. I don't want my issues to become a burden on the people I care about, so I'm doing my best to be there for them, too. \n\nThat's... that's the right way to handle it, isn't it? I just really don't want to make things harder for anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself being constantly on the lookout for potential dangers or threats?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I'm not, like, constantly looking over my shoulder or anything. I really want to just be sensible about it. Does that sound okay? I just don't want to overreact.\n\nDoctor: How did you feel immediately after the fire happened?\n\nPatient: Um... right after? I think... I think I was okay, mostly. \n\nI mean, obviously it was a shock, but I really tried to just hold it together. I wasn't hysterical or crying uncontrollably or anything like that. I really tried my best to be good about it and not cause any extra trouble.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt detached from yourself or your surroundings since the fire?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really detached.
